result3. Command output
# Robotic Mobile Fulfillment Orchestration

An e-commerce fulfillment center uses mobile robots to carry inventory pods to
fixed picking stations. A wave of customer orders has already been released.
The operator must orchestrate pod visits, picking work, robot assignments, and
charging so that every order line is picked while balancing lateness, travel,
energy, congestion, and operational disruption.

You are planning a same-day wave in a robotic mobile fulfillment warehouse.

The goal is to produce a feasible fulfillment plan with low weighted tardiness,
limited robot travel and charging time, balanced use of the stations, low order
fragmentation, low peak-aisle congestion, and tight completion timing for
orders that belong to the same customer promise group.

Hard operational rules:

- Every order line must be picked in exactly the requested quantity.
- A pick can only use inventory from the pod named in the same visit.
- Pod inventory is shared across the whole wave and cannot be overdrawn.
- Each order must be processed at one picking station, although it may use more
  than one pod up to its listed split limit.
- Orders cannot begin before their release slot.
- Cold-chain orders can only be processed at stations certified for cold work.
- Hazmat orders can only be processed at stations certified for hazmat work.
- A robot that handles hazmat work must observe the listed cleaning buffer
  before its next non-hazmat visit.
- Fragile SKUs cannot be picked from heavy pods.
- A station can only handle its listed number of pod visits in a slot.
- A station has a limited number of active open orders over each listed window.
- A pod cannot be in two places at once, and repeat visits to the same pod must
  respect the pod return buffer.
- Robots can only perform non-overlapping visits and charging sessions inside
  their shift.
- Robot maintenance windows are firm downtime; visits and charging sessions
  cannot overlap those intervals.
- Robot battery levels must respect capacity and reserve limits after every
  visit and charging session.
- Charging uses the charger capacity at the chosen station and slot.
- Aisle groups have slot-level traffic limits, especially during the peak wave.
- Orders listed in a customer promise group should finish close together. The
  target spread is not a feasibility cutoff, but excess spread is penalized in
  the operating score.

Data dictionary:

- `config.json`: horizon, slot length, tolerances, cleaning buffer, and scoring
  weights.
- `stations.csv`: station coordinates, certifications, visit capacity, charger
  count, and per-visit picking capacity.
- `station_windows.csv`: station active-order limits over time windows.
- `zones.csv`: storage-zone coordinates and aisle groups.
- `robots.csv`: robot starting zones, battery parameters, charge rates, and
  shift windows.
- `robot_maintenance_windows.csv`: firm robot downtime intervals.
- `skus.csv`: SKU temperature, hazmat, fragile, and handling attributes.
- `pods.csv`: pod home zone, temperature capability, weight class, and return
  buffer.
- `pod_inventory.csv`: available quantity of each SKU on each pod.
- `orders.csv`: order release slot, due slot, priority class, handling class,
  and maximum number of pods that may serve the order.
- `order_lines.csv`: required SKU quantities by order.
- `promise_groups.csv`: customer promise groups and target completion spreads.
- `aisle_capacity.csv`: aisle-group traffic capacity by slot.
- `control_notes.csv`: operational notes from the warehouse control room.

Submission schema:

Write `submissions/solution.csv` with exactly these columns:

```csv
row_type,visit_id,order_id,pod_id,sku_id,robot_id,station_id,start_slot,quantity
```

Use three row types:

- `visit`: defines one pod visit. Fill `visit_id`, `pod_id`, `robot_id`,
  `station_id`, and `start_slot`. Leave `order_id`, `sku_id`, and `quantity`
  blank.
- `pick`: assigns picked quantity to an order line during an existing visit.
  Fill `visit_id`, `order_id`, `sku_id`, and positive `quantity`. The pod,
  robot, station, and start slot are inherited from the visit row and may be
  left blank.
- `charge`: reserves a robot charger. Fill `robot_id`, `station_id`,
  `start_slot`, and `quantity`, where `quantity` is the number of consecutive
  charging slots. Leave `visit_id`, `order_id`, `pod_id`, and `sku_id` blank.

Slots are integer five-minute periods from the start of the planning horizon.
For scoring, an order is complete when the final visit containing one of its
picks has finished.

---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

===== control_notes.csv =====
note_id,note
N1,Cold orders must stay on a station certified for cold-chain handling until the order is complete.
N2,Hazmat picks use the controlled station and force a cleaning gap before the same robot handles ordinary or cold food products.
N3,Peak aisle capacity is tight in the middle of the wave; batching by pod can help but increases order split and lateness risk.
N4,Robot maintenance windows are firm downtime: neither charging nor carrying a pod can overlap those slots.
N5,Customer promise groups reward completing related orders close together rather than letting one order lag far behind the rest.

def load_csv(name):
    with open(f'{base}/{name}', newline='') as f:

    if r['row_type']"'!='"'pick': continue
    v=visits.get(r['visit_id'])
    if not v:
        viol.append((i,'missing_visit'))
        continue
    oid=r['order_id']; sku=r['sku_id']; q=int(r['quantity'])
    if (v['pod_id'],sku) not in pod_inv:
        viol.append((i,'sku_not_on_pod',v['pod_id'],sku))
    if oid not in orders:
        viol.append((i,'bad_order',oid))

    if sku not in skus:

    ord=orders[oid]
    st=v['station_id']
    pod=pods[v['pod_id']]
    skurow=skus[sku]
    if int(ord['release_slot'])>int(v['start_slot']):
        viol.append((i,'before_release',oid,ord['release_slot'],v['start_slot']))
    if ord['temperature_class']=='cold' and st not in {'ST_B','ST_C'}:
        viol.append((i,'cold_at_bad_station',oid,st))
    if ord['hazmat']=='1' and st"'!='"'ST_C':
        viol.append((i,'hazmat_at_bad_station',oid,st))
    if skurow['fragile']=='1' and pod['weight_class']=='heavy':
        viol.append((i,'fragile_from_heavy',sku,v['pod_id']))
print('violations', len(viol))
for v in viol[:50]: print(v)

import csv, math
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
def load_csv(name):
    with open(f'{base}/{name}', newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))
stations={r['station_id']:r for r in load_csv('stations.csv')}
zones={r['zone_id']:r for r in load_csv('zones.csv')}
robots={r['robot_id']:r for r in load_csv('robots.csv')}
pods={r['pod_id']:r for r in load_csv('pods.csv')}
skus={r['sku_id']:r for r in load_csv('skus.csv')}
rows=list(csv.DictReader(open('/app/submissions/solution.csv', newline='')))
visits={r['visit_id']:dict(r,_line=i) for i,r in enumerate(rows, start=2) if r['row_type']=='visit'}
visit_picks=defaultdict(list)
for i,r in enumerate(rows, start=2):
    if r['row_type']=='pick':
        visit_picks[r['visit_id']].append(dict(r,_line=i))

def manhattan(a,b):
    return abs(int(a['x'])-int(b['x']))+abs(int(a['y'])-int(b['y']))
service_min={}
for vid,v in visits.items():
    minutes=sum(int(p['quantity'])*int(skus[p['sku_id']]['unit_pick_time']) for p in visit_picks[vid])
    service_min[vid]=minutes/5.0
# check first-visit earliest arrival and consecutive robot continuity
by_robot=defaultdict(list)
for vid,v in visits.items():
    by_robot[v['robot_id']].append(v)
for lst in by_robot.values():
    lst.sort(key=lambda r:int(r['start_slot']))
print('First-visit earliest arrival lower bounds:')
for rid,lst in sorted(by_robot.items()):
    v=lst[0]
    rz=zones[robots[rid]['start_zone']]
    pz=zones[pods[v['pod_id']]['zone_id']]
    st=stations[v['station_id']]
    lb=(manhattan(rz,pz)+manhattan(pz,st))/5.0
    print(rid,v['visit_id'],'start',v['start_slot'],'lb',lb)
print('\\nRobot gap violations under continuous lower bound:')
count=0
for rid,lst in sorted(by_robot.items()):
    prev_loc=zones[robots[rid]['start_zone']]
    prev_end=0.0
    # pairwise using visit starts as station arrival times; end at pod home after return
    for idx,v in enumerate(lst):
        pz=zones[pods[v['pod_id']]['zone_id']]
        st=stations[v['station_id']]
        t=float(v['start_slot'])
        if idx==0:
            need=(manhattan(prev_loc,pz)+manhattan(pz,st))/5.0
            if t+1e-9 < need:
                print('first',rid,v['visit_id'],'t',t,'need>=',need)
                count+=1
        if idx>0:
            pv=lst[idx-1]
            pst=stations[pv['station_id']]
            ppz=zones[pods[pv['pod_id']]['zone_id']]
            need=service_min[pv['visit_id']] + (manhattan(pst,ppz)+manhattan(ppz,pz)+manhattan(pz,st))/5.0 + int(pods[pv['pod_id']]['return_buffer_slots'])
            gap=t-float(pv['start_slot'])
            if gap+1e-9 < need:
                print(rid,pv['visit_id'],'->',v['visit_id'],'gap',gap,'need>=',need)
                count+=1
print('count',count)
print('\\nPod repeat gap violations:')
by_pod=defaultdict(list)
for vid,v in visits.items():
    by_pod[v['pod_id']].append(v)
count=0
for pid,lst in sorted(by_pod.items()):
    lst.sort(key=lambda r:int(r['start_slot']))
    for i in range(1,len(lst)):
        a,b=lst[i-1],lst[i]
        st_a=stations[a['station_id']]; st_b=stations[b['station_id']]
        pz=zones[pods[pid]['zone_id']]
        need=service_min[a['visit_id']] + (manhattan(st_a,pz)+manhattan(pz,st_b))/5.0 + int(pods[pid]['return_buffer_slots'])
        gap=float(b['start_slot'])-float(a['start_slot'])
        if gap+1e-9 < need:
            print(pid,a['visit_id'],'->',b['visit_id'],'gap',gap,'need>=',need)
            count+=1
print('count',count)

import csv, json
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
def load_csv(name):
    with open(f'{base}/{name}', newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))
config=json.load(open(f'{base}/config.json'))
stations={r['station_id']:r for r in load_csv('stations.csv')}
zones={r['zone_id']:r for r in load_csv('zones.csv')}
robots={r['robot_id']:r for r in load_csv('robots.csv')}
pods={r['pod_id']:r for r in load_csv('pods.csv')}
rows=list(csv.DictReader(open('/app/submissions/solution.csv', newline='')))
visits=[dict(r,_line=i) for i,r in enumerate(rows, start=2) if r['row_type']=='visit']
charges=[dict(r,_line=i) for i,r in enumerate(rows, start=2) if r['row_type']=='charge']
by_robot=defaultdict(list)
for v in visits: by_robot[v['robot_id']].append(v)
for c in charges: by_robot[c['robot_id']].append(c)

def manhattan(a,b): return abs(int(a['x'])-int(b['x']))+abs(int(a['y'])-int(b['y']))
for rid,lst in sorted(by_robot.items()):
    lst.sort(key=lambda r:int(r['start_slot']))
    battery=float(robots[rid]['initial_battery'])
    minb=float(robots[rid]['min_battery'])
    cap=float(robots[rid]['battery_capacity'])
    e=float(robots[rid]['energy_per_distance'])
    charge=float(robots[rid]['charge_per_slot'])
    loc=zones[robots[rid]['start_zone']]
    bad=[]
    for r in lst:
        if r['row_type']=='charge':
            dur=int(r['quantity'])
            battery=min(cap,battery+dur*charge)
            if battery < minb-1e-9 or battery>cap+1e-9:
                bad.append((r['_line'],'charge',battery))
            # loc maybe station? charging at station; after charge robot at station coord? unclear
            loc=stations[r['station_id']]
        else:
            pz=zones[pods[r['pod_id']]['zone_id']]
            st=stations[r['station_id']]
            # lower-bound energy: current loc to pod home + pod home to station + station to pod home
            dist=manhattan(loc,pz)+manhattan(pz,st)+manhattan(st,pz)
            battery-=dist*e
            if battery < minb-1e-9:
                bad.append((r['_line'],r['visit_id'],battery,dist))
            loc=pz
    print(rid,'final_battery',battery,'viol',bad[:10])

        arr=float(v['start_slot'])
        interval=(arr-manhattan(pz,st)/5.0, arr+service+manhattan(st,pz)/5.0)
        overlaps=[m for m in maint[rid] if not (interval[1] <= m[0] or interval[0] >= m[1])]
        if overlaps:
            print(v['visit_id'], interval, overlaps)

stations={r['station_id']:r for r in load_csv('stations.csv')}

counts=defaultdict(int)

viol=[(st,slot,c,int(stations[st]['pod_visits_per_slot'])) for (st,slot),c in sorted(counts.items()) if c>int(stations[st]['pod_visits_per_slot'])]
print('viol',viol)

import csv, math
from collections import defaultdict
base='/app/data'
def load_csv(name):
    with open(f'{base}/{name}', newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))
station_windows=load_csv('station_windows.csv')
skus={r['sku_id']:r for r in load_csv('skus.csv')}
rows=list(csv.DictReader(open('/app/submissions/solution.csv', newline='')))
visits={r['visit_id']:r for r in rows if r['row_type']=='visit'}
visit_service=defaultdict(float)
orders=defaultdict(list)
for r in rows:
    if r['row_type']=='pick':
        visit_service[r['visit_id']]+=int(r['quantity'])*int(skus[r['sku_id']]['unit_pick_time'])/5.0
        v=visits[r['visit_id']]
        orders[r['order_id']].append((float(v['start_slot']), float(v['start_slot'])+visit_service[r['visit_id']], v['station_id'], r['visit_id']))
# rebuild after full service sums
orders2=defaultdict(list)
for r in rows:
    if r['row_type']=='pick':
        v=visits[r['visit_id']]
        orders2[r['order_id']].append((float(v['start_slot']), float(v['start_slot'])+visit_service[r['visit_id']], v['station_id'], r['visit_id']))
intervals=[]
for oid,lst in orders2.items():
    st={x[2] for x in lst}
    if len(st)"'!=1: continue
    s=min(x[0] for x in lst); e=max(x[1] for x in lst); station=next(iter(st))
    intervals.append((oid,station,s,e))
viol=[]
for w in station_windows:
    station=w['"'station_id']; ws=float(w['start_slot']); we=float(w['end_slot']); cap=int(w['max_active_orders'])
    # sweep unique event points within window
    pts={ws,we}
    for oid,st,s,e in intervals:
        if st"'!=station: continue
        pts.add(max(ws,min(we,s)))
        pts.add(max(ws,min(we,e)))
    pts=sorted(pts)
    for a,b in zip(pts,pts[1:]):
        if b<=a: continue
        mid=(a+b)/2
        c=sum(1 for oid,st,s,e in intervals if st==station and s<=mid<e and ws<=mid<we)
        if c>cap:
            viol.append((station,(a,b),c,cap,[oid for oid,st,s,e in intervals if st==station and s<=mid<e]))
            break
print('"'viol',viol)

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

result7. Command output
# Robotic Mobile Fulfillment Orchestration

An e-commerce fulfillment center uses mobile robots to carry inventory pods to
fixed picking stations. A wave of customer orders has already been released.
The operator must orchestrate pod visits, picking work, robot assignments, and
charging so that every order line is picked while balancing lateness, travel,
energy, congestion, and operational disruption.

You are planning a same-day wave in a robotic mobile fulfillment warehouse.

The goal is to produce a feasible fulfillment plan with low weighted tardiness,
limited robot travel and charging time, balanced use of the stations, low order
fragmentation, low peak-aisle congestion, and tight completion timing for
orders that belong to the same customer promise group.

Hard operational rules:

- Every order line must be picked in exactly the requested quantity.
- A pick can only use inventory from the pod named in the same visit.
- Pod inventory is shared across the whole wave and cannot be overdrawn.
- Each order must be processed at one picking station, although it may use more
  than one pod up to its listed split limit.
- Orders cannot begin before their release slot.
- Cold-chain orders can only be processed at stations certified for cold work.
- Hazmat orders can only be processed at stations certified for hazmat work.
- A robot that handles hazmat work must observe the listed cleaning buffer
  before its next non-hazmat visit.
- Fragile SKUs cannot be picked from heavy pods.
- A station can only handle its listed number of pod visits in a slot.
- A station has a limited number of active open orders over each listed window.
- A pod cannot be in two places at once, and repeat visits to the same pod must
  respect the pod return buffer.
- Robots can only perform non-overlapping visits and charging sessions inside
  their shift.
- Robot maintenance windows are firm downtime; visits and charging sessions
  cannot overlap those intervals.
- Robot battery levels must respect capacity and reserve limits after every
  visit and charging session.
- Charging uses the charger capacity at the chosen station and slot.
- Aisle groups have slot-level traffic limits, especially during the peak wave.
- Orders listed in a customer promise group should finish close together. The
  target spread is not a feasibility cutoff, but excess spread is penalized in
  the operating score.

Data dictionary:

- `config.json`: horizon, slot length, tolerances, cleaning buffer, and scoring
  weights.
- `stations.csv`: station coordinates, certifications, visit capacity, charger
  count, and per-visit picking capacity.
- `station_windows.csv`: station active-order limits over time windows.
- `zones.csv`: storage-zone coordinates and aisle groups.
- `robots.csv`: robot starting zones, battery parameters, charge rates, and
  shift windows.
- `robot_maintenance_windows.csv`: firm robot downtime intervals.
- `skus.csv`: SKU temperature, hazmat, fragile, and handling attributes.
- `pods.csv`: pod home zone, temperature capability, weight class, and return
  buffer.
- `pod_inventory.csv`: available quantity of each SKU on each pod.
- `orders.csv`: order release slot, due slot, priority class, handling class,
  and maximum number of pods that may serve the order.
- `order_lines.csv`: required SKU quantities by order.
- `promise_groups.csv`: customer promise groups and target completion spreads.
- `aisle_capacity.csv`: aisle-group traffic capacity by slot.
- `control_notes.csv`: operational notes from the warehouse control room.

Submission schema:

Write `submissions/solution.csv` with exactly these columns:

```csv
row_type,visit_id,order_id,pod_id,sku_id,robot_id,station_id,start_slot,quantity
```

Use three row types:

- `visit`: defines one pod visit. Fill `visit_id`, `pod_id`, `robot_id`,
  `station_id`, and `start_slot`. Leave `order_id`, `sku_id`, and `quantity`
  blank.
- `pick`: assigns picked quantity to an order line during an existing visit.
  Fill `visit_id`, `order_id`, `sku_id`, and positive `quantity`. The pod,
  robot, station, and start slot are inherited from the visit row and may be
  left blank.
- `charge`: reserves a robot charger. Fill `robot_id`, `station_id`,
  `start_slot`, and `quantity`, where `quantity` is the number of consecutive
  charging slots. Leave `visit_id`, `order_id`, `pod_id`, and `sku_id` blank.

Slots are integer five-minute periods from the start of the planning horizon.
For scoring, an order is complete when the final visit containing one of its
picks has finished.

name: or-pyscipopt
description: Use when building, running, validating, or debugging Python optimization models that use PySCIPOpt and SCIP.
metadata:

---

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

FILE: control_notes.csv
note_id,note
N1,Cold orders must stay on a station certified for cold-chain handling until the order is complete.
N2,Hazmat picks use the controlled station and force a cleaning gap before the same robot handles ordinary or cold food products.
N3,Peak aisle capacity is tight in the middle of the wave; batching by pod can help but increases order split and lateness risk.
N4,Robot maintenance windows are firm downtime: neither charging nor carrying a pod can overlap those slots.
N5,Customer promise groups reward completing related orders close together rather than letting one order lag far behind the rest.

---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

import csv, json, math

# load

with open(base+'/pod_inventory.csv', newline='') as f: pinv=list(csv.DictReader(f))
with open(base+'/pods.csv', newline='') as f: pods={r['pod_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/skus.csv', newline='') as f: skus={r['sku_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/stations.csv', newline='') as f: stations={r['station_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
with open(base+'/zones.csv', newline='') as f: zones={r['zone_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(f)}
dem=Counter();
for r in ol: dem[r['sku_id']]+=int(r['quantity'])
inv=Counter();
for r in pinv: inv[r['sku_id']]+=int(r['quantity'])
print('total units demand', sum(dem.values()))
print('total units inv', sum(inv.values()))
print('max demand/inv tight skus:')
for sku in sorted(dem):
    print(sku, dem[sku], inv[sku], round(inv[sku]/dem[sku],2))
# eligible pods per order line by rules
pod_sku=defaultdict(set)
for r in pinv: pod_sku[r['sku_id']].add(r['pod_id'])
forbidden_fragile=set(p for p,row in pods.items() if row['weight_class']=='heavy')
forbidden_temp=defaultdict(set)
for p,row in pods.items():
    cap=row['temperature_capability']

assistant30. Assistant message
The public data supports a concrete reconstruction model if I treat coordinates as Manhattan travel minutes, convert to slots with the five-minute bucket, and let each visit span outbound travel, on-station pick time, and return travel. That gives a defensible event-sweep for robots, pods, stations, chargers, and aisles, and it matches the presence of `unit_pick_time`, battery rates, and station slot capacities.

Plan:
1. Confirm the instance is feasible under that duration model and compute tight eligibility sets, demand aggregation, and simple lower bounds.
2. Build a two-stage approach: a PySCIPOpt allocation/order-assignment model for pod and station decisions, then an exact event-based scheduler/repair layer to place visits, charging, and robot sequences.
3. Add an independent validator from the exported `solution.csv`, run short solves, and iterate only if a concrete violation or clear objective improvement appears.
4. Write `model.md`, `solve.py`, `solution.csv`, and `solve_log.md`, then rerun the generator from scratch and audit the final artifact.

import csv, itertools, math
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
base='/app/data'
orders={r['order_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/orders.csv'))}
lines=defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/order_lines.csv')):
    lines[r['order_id']].append((r['sku_id'], int(r['quantity'])))
pods={r['pod_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/pods.csv'))}
pinv=defaultdict(dict)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/pod_inventory.csv')):
    pinv[r['pod_id']][r['sku_id']]=int(r['quantity'])
skus={r['sku_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/skus.csv'))}
# eligible pods for each line (ignore pod temp capability)
line_pods={}
for o, items in lines.items():
    for sku,q in items:
        elig=[]
        for p in pods:
            if pinv[p].get(sku,0) < q: continue
            if skus[sku]['fragile']=='1' and pods[p]['weight_class']=='heavy': continue
            elig.append(p)
        line_pods[(o,sku)] = elig
# enumerate best pod patterns minimizing num pods then sum local line count score
patterns={}
for o, items in lines.items():
    cand=sorted(set(p for sku,q in items for p in line_pods[(o,sku)]))
    best=None
    for r in range(1, min(len(cand), int(orders[o]['max_split_pods']))+1):
        for subset in itertools.combinations(cand, r):
            ok=True
            assign={}
            for sku,q in items:
                feasible=[p for p in subset if p in line_pods[(o,sku)]]
                if not feasible:
                    ok=False; break
                # choose pod in subset with most coverage
                p=max(feasible, key=lambda x: sum(1 for sku2,_ in items if x in line_pods[(o,sku2)]))
                assign[sku]=p
            if ok:
                score=(r, sum(sorted(Counter(assign.values()).values(), reverse=True)))
                best=(subset, assign)
                break
        if best: break
    patterns[o]=best
# stats
pairs=0
for o,(subset,assign) in patterns.items():
    pairs += len(set(assign.values()))
    print(o, len(set(assign.values())), dict(assign))
print('total order-pod pairs', pairs)

/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import csv, json, math, heapq
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
base='/app/data'
cfg=json.load(open(base+'/config.json'))
slot_minutes=cfg['slot_minutes']
cleanbuf=cfg['cleaning_buffer_slots']
orders={r['order_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/orders.csv'))}
order_lines=defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/order_lines.csv')):
    order_lines[r['order_id']].append({'sku':r['sku_id'],'qty':int(r['quantity'])})
pods={r['pod_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/pods.csv'))}
pod_inv=defaultdict(dict)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/pod_inventory.csv')):
    pod_inv[r['pod_id']][r['sku_id']]=int(r['quantity'])
skus={r['sku_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/skus.csv'))}
stations={r['station_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/stations.csv'))}
zones={r['zone_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/zones.csv'))}
robots={r['robot_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/robots.csv'))}
maint=defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/robot_maintenance_windows.csv')):
    maint[r['robot_id']].append((int(r['start_slot']), int(r['end_slot'])))
for rb in maint: maint[rb].sort()
aisle_cap={(r['aisle_group'],int(r['slot'])):int(r['capacity']) for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/aisle_capacity.csv'))}
station_limits=defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/station_windows.csv')):
    station_limits[r['station_id']].append((int(r['start_slot']),int(r['end_slot']),int(r['max_active_orders'])))
for s in stations:
    station_limits[s].sort()
# travel functions
station_xy={s:(int(r['x']),int(r['y'])) for s,r in stations.items()}
zone_xy={z:(int(r['x']),int(r['y'])) for z,r in zones.items()}
def man(a,b): return abs(a[0]-b[0])+abs(a[1]-b[1])
def loaded_travel_slots(pod, station):
    d=man(zone_xy[pods[pod]['zone_id']], station_xy[station])
    return max(1, math.ceil(d/slot_minutes))
def deadhead_slots(loc_type, loc_id, pod):
    pod_xy=zone_xy[pods[pod]['zone_id']]
    if loc_type=='zone':
        d=man(zone_xy[loc_id], pod_xy)
    else:
        d=man(station_xy[loc_id], pod_xy)
    return math.ceil(d/slot_minutes)
# station assignment simple heuristic
assignment={}
for oid,o in orders.items():
    temp=o['temperature_class']; haz=o['hazmat']=='1'
    if haz: assignment[oid]='ST_C'
    elif temp=='cold': assignment[oid]='ST_B'
    else: assignment[oid]='ST_A'
# line->pod greedy maximizing shared coverage and closeness
linepod={}
for oid, lines in order_lines.items():
    st=assignment[oid]
    candidates=defaultdict(list)
    line_elig={}
    for line in lines:
        sku=line['sku']; q=line['qty']
        elig=[]
        for p in pods:
            if pod_inv[p].get(sku,0) < q: continue
            if skus[sku]['fragile']=='1' and pods[p]['weight_class']=='heavy': continue
            elig.append(p)
        line_elig[sku]=elig
    # greedy by choose pod covering most remaining lines weighted by distance
    remaining={line['sku'] for line in lines}
    while remaining:
        best=None
        for p in pods:
            cover=[sku for sku in remaining if p in line_elig[sku]]
            if not cover: continue
            score=(len(cover), -loaded_travel_slots(p, st), sum(next(line['qty'] for line in lines if line['sku']==sku) for sku in cover))
            if best is None or score>best[0]: best=(score,p,cover)
        _,p,cover=best
        for sku in cover:
            linepod[(oid,sku)] = p
            remaining.remove(sku)
# batch schedule by station
station_orders=defaultdict(list)
for oid, st in assignment.items():
    station_orders[st].append(oid)
for st in station_orders:
    station_orders[st].sort(key=lambda oid:(int(orders[oid]['release_slot']), int(orders[oid]['due_slot']), orders[oid]['order_id']))
station_batch_cap={'ST_A':4,'ST_B':4,'ST_C':3}
station_lanes={s:[0]*int(stations[s]['pod_visits_per_slot']) for s in stations}
active={s:set() for s in stations}
remaining_orders={s:list(v) for s,v in station_orders.items()}
# robot states
rb_state={rb:{'avail':int(r['shift_start']),'loc_type':'zone','loc_id':r['start_zone'],'battery':float(r['initial_battery']),'last_haz':False} for rb,r in robots.items()}
pod_avail={p:0 for p in pods}
aisle_usage=Counter()
visits=[]
order_remaining=defaultdict(lambda:defaultdict(int))
for oid,lines in order_lines.items():
    for line in lines: order_remaining[oid][line['sku']] = line['qty']
order_started={oid:None for oid in orders}
order_completed={}
# active window check conservative via batch cap
H=cfg['planning_horizon_slots']

def order_score(oid, now):
    o=orders[oid]
    pr = 1 if o['priority']=='priority' else 0
    return (pr, -int(o['due_slot']), -int(o['release_slot']))

def add_released_orders(st, now):
    changed=False
    pool=remaining_orders[st]
    while len(active[st]) < station_batch_cap[st]:
        ready=[oid for oid in pool if int(orders[oid]['release_slot']) <= now]
        if not ready: break
        ready.sort(key=lambda oid:(orders[oid]['priority']"'!='"'priority', int(orders[oid]['due_slot']), int(orders[oid]['release_slot']), oid))
        oid=ready[0]
        active[st].add(oid); pool.remove(oid); changed=True
    return changed

def choose_candidate(st, now):
    # return best pod and packed picks for one visit
    maxu=int(stations[st]['max_units_per_visit'])
    cand=[]
    for oid in active[st]:
        for sku,q in order_remaining[oid].items():
            if q<=0: continue
            p=linepod[(oid,sku)]
            cand.append((p,oid,sku,q))
    bypod=defaultdict(list)
    for p,oid,sku,q in cand: bypod[p].append((oid,sku,q))
    best=None
    for p,items in bypod.items():
        items.sort(key=lambda x:(orders[x[0]]['priority']"'!='"'priority', int(orders[x[0]]['due_slot']), x[0], x[1]))
        packed=[]; units=0; pick_minutes=0; haz=False
        finish_orders=set()
        for oid,sku,q in items:
            take=min(q, maxu-units)
            if take<=0: break
            packed.append((oid,sku,take))
            units += take
            pick_minutes += take*int(skus[sku]['unit_pick_time'])
            haz = haz or (orders[oid]['hazmat']=='1')
        if not packed: continue
        dur=2*loaded_travel_slots(p, st) + max(1, math.ceil(pick_minutes/slot_minutes))
        urg=sum((1000 if orders[oid]['priority']=='priority' else 0) + max(0, 60-int(orders[oid]['due_slot'])) for oid,sku,take in packed)
        score=(urg + 20*len({oid for oid,_,_ in packed}) + units*2 - dur, units, -loaded_travel_slots(p,st))
        if best is None or score>best[0]: best=(score,p,packed,dur,haz)
    return best

def earliest_robot_for_visit(pod, st, est, dur, haz):
    best=None
    zone=pods[pod]['zone_id']
    rt_dist=2*man(zone_xy[zone], station_xy[st])
    energy=rt_dist*float(next(iter(robots.values()))['energy_per_distance'])
    for rb,rs in rb_state.items():
        t=max(est, rs['avail'] + deadhead_slots(rs['loc_type'], rs['loc_id'], pod))
        if rs['last_haz'] and not haz:
            t=max(t, rs['avail'] + cleanbuf)
        # avoid maintenance overlap
        changed=True
        while changed:
            changed=False
            for a,b in maint.get(rb,[]):
                if not (t+dur <= a or t >= b):
                    t=b; changed=True
        # aisle capacity availability
        aisle=zones[pods[pod]['zone_id']]['aisle_group']
        while True:
            ok=True
            for tau in range(t, min(H, t+dur)):
                if aisle_usage[(aisle,tau)] + 1 > aisle_cap[(aisle,tau)]:
                    ok=False; t=tau+1; break
            if ok: break
        if t+dur > H: continue
        if rs['battery'] - energy < float(robots[rb]['min_battery']) - 1e-9: continue
        cand=(t, rs['avail'], -rs['battery'], rb, energy)
        if best is None or cand<best[0]: best=(cand, rb, t, energy)
    return best
# simulate by station events
unfinished=True
while unfinished:
    unfinished=False
    progress=False
    for st in ['ST_A','ST_B','ST_C']:
        now=min(station_lanes[st]) if active[st] else min(station_lanes[st]+([min(int(orders[o]['release_slot']) for o in remaining_orders[st])] if remaining_orders[st] else [H]))
        add_released_orders(st, now)
        # if no active, jump lane times to next release maybe
        if not active[st]:
            if remaining_orders[st]:
                nxt=min(int(orders[o]['release_slot']) for o in remaining_orders[st])
                station_lanes[st]=[max(t,nxt) for t in station_lanes[st]]
                add_released_orders(st, nxt)
            else:
                continue
        if active[st]:
            unfinished=True
            lane_idx=min(range(len(station_lanes[st])), key=lambda i: station_lanes[st][i])
            est=station_lanes[st][lane_idx]
            add_released_orders(st, est)
            cand=choose_candidate(st, est)
            if cand is None:
                station_lanes[st][lane_idx]+=1
                continue
            _,pod,packed,dur,haz=cand
            est=max(est, pod_avail[pod], max(int(orders[oid]['release_slot']) for oid,_,_ in packed))
            rob=earliest_robot_for_visit(pod, st, est, dur, haz)
            if rob is None:
                # delay lane a bit
                station_lanes[st][lane_idx]+=1
                continue
            _, rb, start, energy = rob
            end=start+dur
            # commit
            visits.append((st, lane_idx, pod, packed, rb, start, end, haz))
            station_lanes[st][lane_idx]=end
            pod_avail[pod]=end+int(pods[pod]['return_buffer_slots'])
            aisle=zones[pods[pod]['zone_id']]['aisle_group']
            for tau in range(start,end): aisle_usage[(aisle,tau)] += 1
            rs=rb_state[rb]
            rs['avail']=end; rs['loc_type']='zone'; rs['loc_id']=pods[pod]['zone_id']; rs['battery']-=energy; rs['last_haz']=haz
            for oid,sku,take in packed:
                if order_started[oid] is None or start<order_started[oid]: order_started[oid]=start
                order_remaining[oid][sku]-=take
            done_orders=[]
            for oid in list(active[st]):
                if all(q<=0 for q in order_remaining[oid].values()):
                    order_completed[oid]=max(order_completed.get(oid,0), end)
                    done_orders.append(oid)
            for oid in done_orders: active[st].remove(oid)
            progress=True
    if not unfinished: break
    if not progress:
        # advance all lanes one slot to escape stalls
        for st in stations:
            station_lanes[st]=[t+1 for t in station_lanes[st]]
        if min(min(v) for v in station_lanes.values())>H:
            break
# report
feas=all(all(q<=0 for q in order_remaining[o].values()) for o in orders)
print('visits', len(visits), 'feasible orders done', feas, 'completed', len(order_completed))
if not feas:
    for o in orders:
        rem=sum(max(0,q) for q in order_remaining[o].values())
        if rem: print('remaining',o,rem,order_remaining[o])
# capacities summary
print('station lane ends', {s:station_lanes[s] for s in stations})
print('robot battery', {rb:round(st['battery'],1) for rb,st in rb_state.items()})
print('robot avail', {rb:st['avail'] for rb,st in rb_state.items()})
# tardiness
if feas:
    t=0
    for o in orders:
        t += max(0, order_completed[o]-int(orders[o]['due_slot']))
    print('total tardiness', t)

import csv, json, math

# quick estimate durations if travel denominator varies
base='/app/data'

    total=0; numvis=0
    for o,items in lines.items():
        st=assign[o]
        # greedy pod by max coverage
        rem={sku for sku,q in items}
        line_elig={}
        for sku,q in items:
            elig=[]
            for p in pods:
                if pinv[p].get(sku,0) < q: continue
                if skus[sku]['fragile']=='1' and pods[p]['weight_class']=='heavy': continue
                elig.append(p)
            line_elig[sku]=elig
        amap={}
        while rem:
            best=None
            for p in pods:
                cover=[sku for sku in rem if p in line_elig[sku]]
                if not cover: continue
                d=man(zone_xy[pods[p]['zone_id']], station_xy[st])
                score=(len(cover), -math.ceil(d/denom))
                if best is None or score>best[0]: best=(score,p,cover)
            _,p,cover=best
            for sku in cover:
                amap[sku]=p; rem.remove(sku)
        byp=defaultdict(list)
        for sku,q in items: byp[amap[sku]].append((sku,q))
        for p,lst in byp.items():
            units=sum(q for sku,q in lst)
            pick=sum(q*int(skus[sku]['unit_pick_time']) for sku,q in lst)
            d=man(zone_xy[pods[p]['zone_id']], station_xy[st])
            dur=2*max(1,math.ceil(d/denom))+max(1,math.ceil(pick/slot))
            total += dur
            numvis += 1
    print('denom',denom,'order-pod visits',numvis,'avg dur',total/numvis,'total dur',total)

assistant44. Assistant message
The main modeling tradeoff is now clear: batching reduces visits, but it complicates open-order and exact feasibility. For this instance a stronger and safer path is to let SCIP choose station/pod assignments, keep each order’s pod visits serial on a station lane, and then schedule the exact visit sequence with a resource-feasible constructive repair so open-order limits are automatically respected.

maint=defaultdict(list)

    maint[r['robot_id']].append((int(r['start_slot']), int(r['end_slot'])))

aisle_cap={(r['aisle_group'],int(r['slot'])):int(r['capacity']) for r in csv.DictReader(open(base+'/aisle_capacity.csv'))}

def man(a,b): return abs(a[0]-b[0])+abs(a[1]-b[1])
def travel_slots(pod, station):
    d=man(zone_xy[pods[pod]['zone_id']], station_xy[station])
    return max(1, math.ceil(d/TRAVEL_UNITS_PER_SLOT))
def deadhead_slots(loc_type, loc_id, pod):
    pod_xy=zone_xy[pods[pod]['zone_id']]
    if loc_type=='zone': d=man(zone_xy[loc_id], pod_xy)
    else: d=man(station_xy[loc_id], pod_xy)
    return math.ceil(d/TRAVEL_UNITS_PER_SLOT)
# simple station assign and pod choice
assign={o:('ST_C' if orders[o]['hazmat']=='1' else 'ST_B' if orders[o]['temperature_class']=='cold' else 'ST_A') for o in orders}
linepod={}
for oid, lines in order_lines.items():
    st=assign[oid]
    rem={line['sku'] for line in lines}
    elig={}
    for line in lines:
        sku,q=line['sku'],line['qty']
        L=[]
        for p in pods:
            if pod_inv[p].get(sku,0) < q: continue
            if skus[sku]['fragile']=='1' and pods[p]['weight_class']=='heavy': continue
            L.append(p)
        elig[sku]=L
    while rem:
        best=None
        for p in pods:
            cover=[sku for sku in rem if p in elig[sku]]
            if not cover: continue
            score=(len(cover), -travel_slots(p,st))
            if best is None or score>best[0]: best=(score,p,cover)
        _,p,cover=best
        for sku in cover:
            linepod[(oid,sku)] = p
            rem.remove(sku)

poduse=Counter(linepod.values())
for oid,lines in order_lines.items():
    st=assign[oid]
    byp=defaultdict(list)
    for line in lines: byp[linepod[(oid,line['sku'])]].append((line['sku'],line['qty']))
    visits=[]
    cap=int(stations[st]['max_units_per_visit'])
    for p,items in byp.items():
        total=sum(q for sku,q in items)
        # split if needed
        chunks=[]
        current=[]; units=0
        for sku,q in items:
            while q>0:
                take=min(q, cap-units)
                current.append((sku,take))
                units += take; q -= take
                if units==cap:
                    chunks.append(current); current=[]; units=0
        if current: chunks.append(current)
        for chunk in chunks:
            pick=sum(q*int(skus[sku]['unit_pick_time']) for sku,q in chunk)
            dur=2*travel_slots(p,st)+max(1,math.ceil(pick/slot_minutes))
            visits.append({'pod':p,'picks':chunk,'dur':dur,'haz':orders[oid]['hazmat']=='1','busy':poduse[p]})
    visits.sort(key=lambda v:(-v['busy'],-v['dur'],v['pod']))
    order_visits[oid]=visits

station_orders=defaultdict(list)

pod_avail={p:0 for p in pods}
aisle_use=Counter();
visit_rows=[]
completion={}
station_blocks={s:[0]*int(stations[s]['pod_visits_per_slot']) for s in stations}
def robot_options(pod, st, earliest, dur, haz):
    zone=pods[pod]['zone_id']; d=man(zone_xy[zone], station_xy[st]); energy=2*d*float(next(iter(robots.values()))['energy_per_distance'])
    opts=[]
    for rb,rs in rb_state.items():
        t=max(earliest, rs['avail'] + deadhead_slots(rs['loc_type'], rs['loc_id'], pod))
        if rs['last_haz'] and not haz:
            t=max(t, rs['avail'] + cleanbuf + deadhead_slots(rs['loc_type'], rs['loc_id'], pod))
        while True:
            moved=False
            for a,b in maint.get(rb,[]):
                if not (t+dur <= a or t >= b):
                    t=b; moved=True; break
            if not moved: break
        if t+dur>cfg['planning_horizon_slots']: continue
        if rs['battery'] - energy < float(robots[rb]['min_battery'])-1e-9: continue
        opts.append((t, -rs['battery'], rb, energy))
    return sorted(opts)
for st in ['ST_C','ST_B','ST_A']:
    lanes=station_blocks[st]
    for oid in station_orders[st]:
        lane=min(range(len(lanes)), key=lambda i: lanes[i])
        t=max(lanes[lane], int(orders[oid]['release_slot']))
        start_block=None
        for vis in order_visits[oid]:
            pod=vis['pod']; dur=vis['dur']; haz=vis['haz']; aisle=zones[pods[pod]['zone_id']]['aisle_group']
            t=max(t, pod_avail[pod])
            while True:
                # aisle and station lane reserved by block so no station check needed beyond lane
                ok=True
                for tau in range(t, t+dur):
                    if tau>=cfg['planning_horizon_slots'] or aisle_use[(aisle,tau)] + 1 > aisle_cap[(aisle,tau)]:
                        ok=False; t=tau+1; break
                if not ok: continue
                opts=robot_options(pod, st, t, dur, haz)
                if not opts:
                    t += 1; continue
                t2, _, rb, energy = opts[0]
                if t2>t:
                    t=t2; continue
                if start_block is None: start_block=t
                end=t+dur
                visit_rows.append((oid, st, lane, pod, vis['picks'], rb, t, end, haz))
                aisle=zones[pods[pod]['zone_id']]['aisle_group']
                for tau in range(t,end): aisle_use[(aisle,tau)] += 1
                pod_avail[pod]=end+int(pods[pod]['return_buffer_slots'])
                rs=rb_state[rb]; rs['avail']=end; rs['loc_type']='zone'; rs['loc_id']=pods[pod]['zone_id']; rs['battery']-=energy; rs['last_haz']=haz
                t=end
                break
        lanes[lane]=t
        completion[oid]=t
# report
print('max lane end', {s:max(v) for s,v in station_blocks.items()})
print('completed within horizon', all(t<=cfg['planning_horizon_slots'] for t in completion.values()), len(completion))
print('max completion', max(completion.values()))
print('robot battery', {rb:round(st['battery'],1) for rb,st in rb_state.items()})

print('tardiness', late)
